\displaystyle-2\mu_{0}(E)H(E)\int_{E}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}\Bigg\}+\lambda^{2}\big[\cdots\big]+\lambda^{3}\int_{E}(\pazocal{S}H)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}H\geq 0.

Assume that
\lambda>0
and divide both sides of the previous inequality by this factor and make
\lambda\to 0
to obtain the desired estimate. Let us notice that the kernel associated to
\pazocal{S}
is continuous, meaning that the terms in
[\cdots]
and
\int_{E}(\pazocal{S}H)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}H
are finite, since
H(E)<\infty
due to the fact that
\mu_{\lambda}\in\Sigma_{n}(E)
for any
\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{0}]
with
\lambda_{0}>0
.
∎

We remark that in the particular case we will apply this lemma,
E
will be a finite union of cubes, so
\mu_{0}(E)>0
. Moreover, we will choose a specific measure
H
so that
H(E)>0
. Therefore, in this setting, the inequality in Lemma
4.3
can be rewritten as

(4.1)

\frac{\mu_{0}(E)+2\int_{E}|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mu_{0}(E)}\leq\frac{1}{H(E)}\int_{E}\Big(|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}+2\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})\Big)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}H,

which is a similar estimate that resembles that of
[
Vo
, Lemma 5.6]
.

We shall apply (
4.1
) to prove an auxiliary lemma, but first let us introduce the following notation for a certain maximal function applied to a measure:

M\nu(\overline{x}):=\sup_{r>0}\frac{\nu(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}}

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
E
be a finite union of cubes. Then, for the potential

\pazocal{U}^{\mu_{0}}:=M\mu_{0}+\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0},

there is a constant
\alpha_{0}>0
such that

\pazocal{U}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0},\quad\forall\overline{x}\in E.

Proof.

Choose
\alpha_{1}\in(0,80^{-n})
. If
\overline{x}_{0}\in E
is such that
M\mu_{0}(\overline{x}_{0})\geq\alpha_{1}
then we are done at
\overline{x}_{0}
. So assume
M\mu_{0}(\overline{x}_{0})<\alpha_{1}
. Pick
\varepsilon_{1}\leq\tau_{0},R_{0}:=\varepsilon_{1}/10
and
B_{0}:=B_{R_{0}}(\overline{x}_{0})
. Set
\mu_{00}:=\mu_{0}|_{2B_{0}}
and define

G:=\big\{\overline{y}\in B_{0}\cap E\,:\,M\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\leq 4^{-n}\big\}.

We shall prove that the complement of
G
in
B_{0}\cap E
is small. If
\overline{y}\in(B_{0}\cap E)\setminus{G}
, then there exists
r=r(\overline{y})>0
so that

\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{y}))}{r^{n}}>4^{-n}.

If we had
r>\varepsilon_{1}/20=R_{0}/2
, then

\frac{1}{80^{n}}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{y}))}{20^{n}r^{n}}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{20r}(\overline{x}_{0}))}{(20r)^{n}}<\alpha_{1},\quad\text{since}\;M\mu_{0}(\overline{x}_{0})<\alpha_{1},

and this cannot be. Then
r\leq\varepsilon_{1}/20
, implying
B_{r}(\overline{y})\cap(2B_{0})=B_{r}(\overline{y})
, which in turn implies
\mu_{00}(B_{r}(\overline{y}))=\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{y}))
. So we have found

(4.2)

\forall\overline{y}\in(B_{0}\cap E)\setminus{G},\,\exists r(\overline{y})>0\;\text{ such that }\;\frac{\mu_{00}(B_{r}(\overline{y}))}{r^{n}}>4^{-n}.

We continue by choosing
\varepsilon_{1}
(that was already smaller than
\tau_{0}
) small enough so that

\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{0}\cap E)\geq a(n)R_{0}^{n+1}=:aR_{0}^{n+1},

for some dimensional constant
a>0
. This can be done since
E
is a finite union of cubes. Notice also that the dependence on
E
of the previous estimate is in
R_{0}
, since
\varepsilon_{1}
depends on
\tau_{0}
, that depends on
E
. Using (
4.2
) and
[
Ma
, Theorem 2.1]
we obtain a countable covering
\{B_{5r_{j}}(\overline{y}_{j})\}
of
(B_{0}\cap E)\setminus{G}
with
\{B_{r_{j}}(\overline{y}_{j})\}
disjoint and also satisfying (
4.2
). Now,

\displaystyle\pazocal{L}^{n+1}\big((B_{0}\cap E)\setminus{G}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 5^{n+1}\sum_{j}r_{j}^{n+1}\leq 2\cdot 5^{n+1}R_{0}\sum_{j}r_{j}^{n}

\displaystyle<10\cdot 4^{n}\cdot 5^{n+1}R_{0}\sum_{j}\mu_{00}\big(B_{r_{j}}(\overline{y}_{j})\big)=10\cdot 20^{n}R_{0}\,\mu_{00}\bigg(\bigcup_{j}B_{r_{j}}(\overline{y}_{j})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 10\cdot 20^{n}R_{0}\,\mu_{0}(2B_{0})\leq 10\cdot 40^{n}R_{0}^{n+1}\alpha_{1}=:A(n)R_{0}^{n+1}\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A}{a}\alpha_{1}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{0}\cap E),

where we have applied the hypothesis
M\mu_{0}(\overline{x}_{0})<\alpha_{1}
. Therefore, by definition of
G
,

\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(G)\geq\bigg(\frac{a}{A\alpha_{1}}-1\bigg)\pazocal{L}^{n+1}\big((B_{0}\cap E)\setminus{G}\big).

We pick
\alpha_{1}\in(0,80^{-n})
depending only on
n
so that

\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(G):=\pazocal{L}^{n+1}\big(\big\{\overline{y}\in B_{0}\cap E\,:\,M\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\leq 4^{-n}\big\}\big)>0.

We will now prove that for all sufficiently small
\lambda>0
,
\mu_{0}+\lambda\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{G}
belongs to
\Sigma_{n}(E)
, i.e.

M\big(\mu_{0}+\lambda\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{G}\big)\leq 1,\quad\text{on }\,E\,\text{ if }\,\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0}).

Let us fix
\overline{z}\in E,r>0
and write
\mu_{\lambda}:=\mu_{0}+\lambda\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{G}
. Distinguish three cases: first, if
B_{r}(\overline{z})\cap G=\varnothing
then it is clear that

\frac{\mu_{\lambda}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}=\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}\leq 1,

that is the desired estimate. If
B_{r}(\overline{z})\cap G\neq\varnothing
and
\overline{z}\in G
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{\lambda}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}=\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}+\lambda\frac{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{r}(\overline{z})\cap G)}{r^{n}}\leq\frac{1}{4^{n}}+\lambda\frac{\min\big\{r^{n+1},R_{0}^{n+1}\big\}}{r^{n}}.

If
r\leq R_{0}
, then

\frac{\mu_{\lambda}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}\leq\frac{1}{4^{n}}+\lambda r\leq\frac{1}{4^{n}}+\lambda R_{0}<1,\quad\text{for $\lambda$ small enough}.

If on the other hand
r>R_{0}
, we also have

\frac{\mu_{\lambda}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}\leq\frac{1}{4^{n}}+\lambda\frac{R_{0}^{n+1}}{r^{n}}\leq\frac{1}{4^{n}}+\lambda R_{0}<1,\quad\text{for $\lambda$ small enough}.

Finally, for the third case, that is, if
B_{r}(\overline{z})\cap G\neq\varnothing
and
\overline{z}\not\in G
, take
\overline{y}\in B_{r}(\overline{z})\cap G
so that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{\lambda}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}+\lambda\frac{\min\big\{r^{n+1},R_{0}^{n+1}\big\}}{r^{n}}\leq 2^{n}\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{2r}(\overline{y}))}{(2r)^{n}}+\lambda R_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{n}}+(2^{n}+1)\lambda R_{0},

where for the last inequality we have applied the reasoning of the second case. Hence, in general, we deduce that for
\lambda>0
small enough,
\mu_{\lambda}\in\Sigma_{n}(E)
.

Now we argue as follows: choose
\varepsilon_{1}^{(k)}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
a sequence converging to 0. For each
k
set
R_{0}^{(k)}:=\varepsilon_{1}^{(k)}/10
and build a set
G_{k}
in
B_{R_{0}^{(k)}}(\overline{x}_{0})
as above. Let
H_{k}:=\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{G_{k}}
and apply Lemma
4.2
, or in this particular case (
4.1
), to get

1\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(E)+2\int_{E}|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mu_{0}(E)}\leq\frac{1}{H_{k}(E)}\int_{E}\Big(|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}+2\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})\Big)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}H_{k}.

Since the measures
H_{k}/H_{k}(E)
converge weakly to a point mass probability measure at
\overline{x}_{0}
, by the continuity of the kernel associated to
\pazocal{S}
we have

1\leq|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}(\overline{x}_{0})+2\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x}_{0})\quad\rightarrow\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}(\overline{x}_{0})+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x}_{0}).

Therefore, either
|\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}|^{2}(\overline{x}_{0})\geq 1/4
or
\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x}_{0})\geq 1/4
, so in any case we get

\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{x}_{0})+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x}_{0})\geq\frac{1}{4},

by the nonnegativity of each term. Since this last estimate follows if
M\mu_{0}(\overline{x}_{0})
is smaller than
\alpha_{1}
, chosen to depend only on
n
, setting
\alpha_{0}:=\min\{\alpha_{1},1/4\}
the result follows.
∎

4.1.
The construction

Both of the above lemmas are enough to carry out the construction of the desired cubes in a similar way to
[
Vo
, pp. 38-42]
. Fix
E\subset B_{1}(0)\subset 2Q_{0}
compact consisting of a finite union of cubes, and define the auxiliary potential

\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x}):=\pazocal{U}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x})+M(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0})(\overline{x}).

Apply Lemma
4.3
to
E
and obtain its corresponding constant
\alpha_{0}
, and pick
0<\beta\ll 1
depending only on
n
, to be fixed later on, and set

\pazocal{G}:=\big\{\overline{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\,:\,\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{y})>\beta\alpha_{0}\big\}\supset E.

Consider
\{Q_{j}\}_{j}
a Whitney decomposition of
\pazocal{G}
(see
[
St
, pp. 167-169]
, for example), that is: a countable family of closed dyadic cubes with disjoint interiors that cover
\pazocal{G}
and such that for some constant
A>0
,

•

20Q_{j}\subset\pazocal{G}
for each
j
,

•

(AQ_{j})\cap\pazocal{G}^{c}\neq\varnothing
for each
j
,

•

The family
\{10Q_{j}\}_{j}
has bounded overlap. Moreover, if
10Q_{j}\cap 10Q_{i}\neq\varnothing
, then
\ell(Q_{j})\approx\ell(Q_{i})
.

From those
Q_{j}
satisfying
\frac{5}{4}Q_{j}\cap E\neq\varnothing
, choose a finite subcovering and enumerate them as
Q_{j_{1}},\ldots,Q_{j_{N}}
. We shall check that

\pazocal{Q}_{i}:=2Q_{j_{i}},\quad i=1,\ldots,N

is the desired family of cubes that satisfies the properties in Theorem
4.1
. It is clear that properties
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{1}}
and
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{5}}
are satisfied by construction, so we are only left to verify
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
,
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
and
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}
. The first two will be checked for
\gamma_{\text{sy},+}
instead of
\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}
, which is enough due to the comparability of both capacities.

Proof of
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
in Theorem
4.1
.

We shall prove that property
\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x})\geq\beta\alpha_{0}
on
\pazocal{F}
, implies

\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(\pazocal{F})\leq\frac{C}{\beta\alpha_{0}}\mu_{0}(E),

where
\mu_{0}
is an extremal measure for
\gamma_{\text{var}}(E)
and
C>0
constant. If this is the case, we are done, since we have

\gamma_{\text{sy},+}(\pazocal{F})\approx\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(\pazocal{F})\leq\frac{C}{\beta\alpha_{0}}\gamma_{\text{var}}(E)\leq\frac{C}{\beta\alpha_{0}}\gamma_{\text{sy},\psi_{\tau_{0}},2}(E)\leq\frac{C}{\beta\alpha_{0}}\,2C_{1}C_{2}\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(E)\approx\gamma_{\text{sy},+}(E),

where for the third estimate recall Remark
3.4
and the fact that relation (
3.10
) holds in general, and in the fourth inequality we have used the definition of
\tau_{0}
.

So let us choose
\mu
admissible for
\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(\pazocal{F})
with
\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(\pazocal{F})\leq 2\mu(\pazocal{F})
and observe that

\|\pazocal{S}_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)\to L^{2}(\mu)}\leq\|\pazocal{P}_{\text{sy},\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)\to L^{2}(\mu)}\leq 1,

since
P_{\text{{sy}}}
and the measure
\mu
are nonnegative. Therefore,
\pazocal{S}_{\mu}
becomes a CZ convolution operator (with continuous kernel), so that it satisfies the weak estimate

\mu\big(\{\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\geq\lambda\}\big)\leq C^{\prime}\frac{\mu_{0}(E)}{\lambda},

where
C^{\prime}>0
is a constant. By definition, for each
\overline{x}\in\pazocal{F}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle:=M\mu_{0}(\overline{x})+\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{x})+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})+M(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})

\displaystyle=:\text{I}(\overline{x})+\text{II}(\overline{x})+\text{III}(\overline{x})+\text{IV}(\overline{x}),

so at least one of the four terms is larger than
\frac{\beta\alpha_{0}}{4}=:a
. In fact, if we name

\displaystyle\pazocal{F}_{1}:=\big\{\overline{x}\in F\,:\,\text{I}(\overline{x})\geq a\big\},

\displaystyle\qquad\pazocal{F}_{2}:=\big\{\overline{x}\in F\,:\,\text{II}(\overline{x})\geq a\big\},

\displaystyle\pazocal{F}_{3}:=\big\{\overline{x}\in F\,:\,\text{III}(\overline{x})\geq a\big\},

\displaystyle\qquad\pazocal{F}_{4}:=\big\{\overline{x}\in F\,:\,\text{IV}(\overline{x})\geq a\big\},

we have
\pazocal{F}=\cup_{i=1}^{4}\pazocal{F}_{i}
. So in particular
\mu(\pazocal{F})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mu(\pazocal{F}_{i})
and then, necessarily, there exists
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
such that
\mu(\pazocal{F}_{i})\geq\mu(\pazocal{F})/4
. Let us study separately each possibility:

a
)

If
i=1
. In this case
M\mu_{0}\geq a
on
\pazocal{F}_{1}
with
\mu(\pazocal{F}_{1})\geq\mu(\pazocal{F})/4
. For each
\overline{x}\in\pazocal{F}_{1}
choose a ball
B_{\overline{x}}:=B_{r(\overline{x})}(\overline{x})
so that
\mu_{0}(B_{\overline{x}})\geq ar(\overline{x})^{n}
. Apply Besicovitch’s covering theorem
[
Ma
, Theorem 2.7]
to extract a countable collection of balls
\{B_{\overline{x}_{j}}\}_{j}
with bounded multiplicity of overlapping (with constant depending only on the dimension) that covers
\pazocal{F}_{1}
. Then,

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\frac{\mu(\pazocal{F})}{4}\leq\mu(\pazocal{F}_{1})\leq\sum_{j}\mu(B_{\overline{x}_{j}})\leq\sum_{j}r^{n}(\overline{x}_{j})\leq\frac{1}{a}\sum_{j}\mu_{0}(B_{\overline{x}_{j}})\lesssim\frac{\mu_{0}(\pazocal{F}_{1})}{a}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(E)}{a},

since
\mu_{0}
is supported on
E
.

b
)

If
i=2
. Now
\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\geq a
on
\pazocal{F}_{2}
with
\mu(\pazocal{F}_{2})\geq\mu(\pazocal{F})/4
. Using the weak estimate presented above we get the desired estimate

\frac{\mu(\pazocal{F})}{4}\leq\mu(\pazocal{F}_{2})=\mu\big(\{\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\geq a\}\big)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{a}\mu_{0}(E).

c
)

If
i=3
. Now
\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})\geq a
on
\pazocal{F}_{3}
with
\mu(\pazocal{F}_{3})\geq\mu(\pazocal{F})/4
. By (
3.10
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mu(\pazocal{F})}{4}\leq\mu(\pazocal{F}_{3})

\displaystyle=\mu\big(\{\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})\geq a\}\big)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{a}\int_{E}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{a}\mu_{0}(E)^{1/2}\bigg(\int_{E}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}\bigg)^{2}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{a}\mu_{0}(E).

d
)

If
i=4
. Now
M(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0})\geq a
on
\pazocal{F}_{4}
with
\mu(\pazocal{F}_{4})\geq\mu(\pazocal{F})/4
. Proceeding analogously as in the first case, we deduce

\frac{\mu(\pazocal{F})}{4}\lesssim\frac{1}{a}\int_{E}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0},

and arguing as in the third case we obtain the desired result.

∎

To prove
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
we will need the following lemma:

Lemma 4.4
.

For
i=1,\ldots,N
define
\mu_{i}:=\mu_{0}|_{5Q_{j_{i}}}
and consider the auxiliary potential

\pazocal{W}^{\mu_{i}}:=M\mu_{i}+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{i}}1+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{i}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})+M(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{i}).

Then, there exists a constant
\alpha_{0}^{\prime}>0
such that for each
i=1,\ldots,N
,

\pazocal{W}^{\mu_{i}}(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0}^{\prime}\alpha_{0},\qquad\forall\overline{x}\in 4Q_{j_{i}}\cap E.

Proof.

For the sake of notation, in this proof we rename
Q_{i}:=Q_{j_{i}}
. Fix
i=1,\ldots,N
,
\overline{x}\in 4Q_{i}\cap E
and
\overline{z}\in(AQ_{i})\cap\pazocal{G}^{c}
, which is non-empty by construction. So in particular

(4.3)

M\mu_{0}(\overline{z})\leq\beta\alpha_{0},\quad\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{z})\leq\beta\alpha_{0}.

Now choosing, for example,
R:=2(4+A)\text{diam}(Q_{i})
it is clear that we have

|\overline{x}-\overline{z}|\leq(4+A)\text{diam}(Q_{i})\leq\frac{R}{2}.

Therefore, using the CZ estimates for
P_{\text{{sy}}}\psi_{\tau_{0}}
, the nonnegativity of the latter kernel and relations (
4.3
) we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{R}\mu_{0}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle:=\int_{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|>R}P_{\text{{sy}}}(\overline{x}-\overline{y})\psi_{\tau_{0}}(\overline{x}-\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\alpha_{0}+\int_{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|>R}\Big\rvert P_{\text{{sy}}}(\overline{x}-\overline{y})\psi_{\tau_{0}}(\overline{x}-\overline{y})-P_{\text{{sy}}}(\overline{z}-\overline{y})\psi_{\tau_{0}}(\overline{z}-\overline{y})\Big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})

\displaystyle\lesssim\beta\alpha_{0}+|\overline{x}-\overline{z}|\int_{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|>R}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n+1}}\lesssim\beta\alpha_{0}+|\overline{x}-\overline{z}|\int_{|\overline{z}-\overline{y}|>R/4}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})}{|\overline{z}-\overline{y}|^{n+1}}

(4.4)

\displaystyle\leq\beta\alpha_{0}+\frac{R}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{2^{j+1}R}(\overline{z}))}{(2^{j}R)^{n+1}}\leq\beta\alpha_{0}+\frac{R}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{\beta\alpha_{0}(2^{j+1}R)^{n}}{(2^{j}R)^{n+1}}\lesssim\beta\alpha_{0}.

We also notice that there exists
\widetilde{A}=\widetilde{A}(n)>0
such that

(4.5)

M\mu_{0}(\overline{x})\leq\max\big\{\widetilde{A}\,\beta\alpha_{0},M\mu_{i}(\overline{x})\big\}.

Indeed, if
r\geq\ell(Q_{i})/20
then

\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{40\sqrt{n}Ar}(\overline{z}))}{r^{n}}\leq\widetilde{A}(n)\beta\alpha_{0}.

If
r<\ell(Q_{i})/20
use that
\overline{x}\in 4Q_{i}
and
\mu_{i}:=\mu_{0}|_{5Q_{i}}
to simply have

\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}}=\frac{\mu_{j}(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}},

so indeed
M\mu_{0}(\overline{x})\leq\max\big\{\widetilde{A}\,\beta\alpha_{0},M\mu_{i}(\overline{x})\big\}
. Having made this observations, we recall that by Lemma
4.3
we always have

M\mu_{0}+\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}+\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})\geq\alpha_{0},\quad\text{on }\,E.

Now we distinguish three cases:

a
)

If
M\mu_{0}(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0}/3
. Then (
4.5
) implies

\frac{\alpha_{0}}{3}\leq\max\big\{\widetilde{A}\,\beta\alpha_{0},M\mu_{i}(\overline{x})\big\},

so choosing
\beta\leq(6\widetilde{A})^{-1}
we deduce
M\mu_{i}(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0}/6
and we would be done.

b
)

If
\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0}/3
, by (
4.4
) there exists
C>0
constant such that

\pazocal{S}\big(\mu|_{B_{R}(\overline{x})}\big)(\overline{x})\geq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{3}-C\beta\alpha_{0}\geq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4},

for
\beta
small enough. In addition, by definition of
R
,

\pazocal{S}\big(\mu|_{B_{R}(\overline{x})\setminus{5Q_{i}}}\big)(\overline{x})\leq C^{\prime}\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{2R}(\overline{z}))}{\ell(Q_{i})^{n}}\leq 2^{n}C^{\prime}\beta\alpha_{0}\frac{R^{n}}{\ell(Q_{i})^{n}}=C^{\prime\prime}\beta\alpha_{0}.

Hence

\pazocal{S}\mu_{i}(\overline{x})=\pazocal{S}\big(\mu_{0}|_{5Q_{i}}\big)(\overline{x})\geq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}-C^{\prime\prime}\beta\alpha_{0}\geq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{5},

for
\beta
small enough, and we are done.

c
)

If
\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0}/3
. Let us observe that for
\overline{z}\in(AQ_{i})\cap\pazocal{G}^{c}
, by definition of
\pazocal{G}
we also have

M(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0})(\overline{z})\leq\beta\alpha_{0},\qquad\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{z})\leq\beta\alpha_{0}.

So choosing again
R:=2(4+A)\text{diam}(Q_{i})
we would be able to prove analogously as in (
4.4
) that

\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0},R}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})\leq C\beta\alpha_{0},

for some
C>0
constant. Moreover,

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}|_{B_{R}(\overline{x})\setminus{5Q_{i}}}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})\leq C^{\prime}\frac{\int_{B_{2R}(\overline{z})}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\ell(Q_{i})^{n}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\beta\alpha_{0},

where we have used, again, the definition of
R
and that
M(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0})(\overline{z})\leq\beta\alpha_{0}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{i}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})

\displaystyle=\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})-\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}|_{B_{R}(\overline{x})\setminus{5Q_{i}}}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})-\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0},R}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{3}-C^{\prime\prime}\beta\alpha_{0}-C\beta\alpha_{0}\geq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{5},

for
\beta
small enough, and the proof is completed.

∎

Proof of
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
in Theorem
4.1
.

Repeating the same arguments presented for the proof of
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
, one deduces that property
\pazocal{W}^{\mu_{i}}(\overline{x})\geq\alpha_{0}^{\prime}\alpha
, for every
\overline{x}\in 4Q_{j_{i}}\cap E
and every
i=1,\ldots,N
, implies

\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(4Q_{j_{i}}\cap E)\leq\frac{C}{\alpha_{0}^{\prime}\alpha_{0}}\bigg(\mu_{0}(5Q_{j_{i}})+\int_{5Q_{j_{i}}}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}\bigg).

But notice that the bounded multiplicity of overlapping of the family
\{10Q_{j}\}_{j}
and the fact that
\mu_{0}
is supported on
E
yield

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigg(\mu_{0}(Q_{j_{i}})+\int_{5Q_{j_{i}}}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}\bigg)\lesssim\mu_{0}(E)+\int_{E}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}\leq 2\mu_{0}(E),

where for the last inequality we have used (
3.10
). Therefore, by the latter relation and using the definition of
\tau_{0}
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\gamma_{\text{sy},+}(4Q_{j_{i}}\cap E)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N}\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(4Q_{j_{i}}\cap E)\lesssim\mu_{0}(E)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\text{var}}(E)\leq\gamma_{\text{sy},\psi_{\tau_{0}},2}(E)\leq 2C_{1}C_{2}\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(E)\lesssim\gamma_{\text{sy},+}(E),

that is the desired estimate.
∎

Finally, we prove
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}
. Let us first observe that the assumption

\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E)\leq C_{+}\text{diam}(E)^{n},\quad\text{ for $C_{+}>0$ constant to be fixed below},

is not at all restrictive. Indeed, if it failed,
[
MPr
, Lemma 4.1]
would imply

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\leq\gamma(E)\lesssim\pazocal{H}_{\infty}^{n}(E)\leq\text{diam}(E)^{n}<\frac{1}{C_{+}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E),

and we would be done.

Proof of
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}
in Theorem
4.1
.

Fix
\overline{x}\not\in E
and observe that the following estimates hold

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(E)}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}},

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{\mu_{0}}(\pazocal{S}\mu_{0})(\overline{x})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}}\int_{E}\int_{E}P_{\text{{sy}}}(\overline{y}-\overline{z})\psi_{\tau_{0}}(\overline{y}-\overline{z})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{z})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}}\int_{E}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(E)}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}},

where for the last inequality we have applied (
3.10
). Since
\text{supp}(\mu_{0})\subset E
, we have

\sup_{r>0}\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}}=\sup_{r>\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)}\frac{\mu_{0}(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(E)}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}},

and moreover, due to (
3.10
) again,

\displaystyle\sup_{r>0}\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{B_{r}(\overline{x})}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})

\displaystyle=\sup_{r>\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)}\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{B_{r}(\overline{x})}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}}\int_{E}\pazocal{S}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}(\overline{y})\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(E)}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}}.

All in all, we get

\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x})\leq\frac{4\mu_{0}(E)}{\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)}.

Let us pick
\overline{x}\in\pazocal{G}\cap E^{c}
(which is non empty, since
E\subset\pazocal{G}
with
\pazocal{G}
open, by the lower semicontinuity of the potential
\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}
) so that
\widetilde{\pazocal{U}}^{\mu_{0}}(\overline{x})>\beta\alpha_{0}
and observe that using the definition of
\mu_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
as in the previous proofs, we get

\displaystyle\text{dist}(\overline{x},E)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C_{1}C_{2}}{\alpha_{0}\beta}\gamma_{\text{sy},\text{op}}(E)\leq\frac{8CC_{1}C_{2}}{\alpha_{0}\beta}\gamma_{\text{sy},+}(E)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16CC_{1}C_{2}}{\alpha_{0}\beta}\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E)\leq\frac{16CC_{1}C_{2}}{\alpha_{0}\beta}C_{+}\text{diam}(E)^{n}.

Choosing
C_{+}
appropriately to neglect the effect of all the above constants that have already been fixed, the result follows. Indeed, this is because the previous estimate is valid for all
\overline{x}\in\pazocal{G}\cap E^{c}
, implying that one can make
\partial\pazocal{G}
say
\frac{\text{diam}(E)}{1000}
close to
E
. Since we also know that
20Q_{j}\subset\pazocal{G}
we have

\text{diam}(2Q_{j})\leq\frac{1}{10}\text{dist}(\partial\pazocal{G},Q_{j}),

where
\text{dist}(\partial\pazocal{G},Q_{j})
is comparable to
\text{dist}(\partial\pazocal{G},E)
since the cubes
Q_{j}
have been also chosen so that
\frac{5}{4}Q_{j}\cap E\neq\varnothing
.
∎

5.
A comparability result under an additional assumption

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be a compact set. As it is stated in
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}
in Theorem
4.1
, we shall work under the following additional assumption:

\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{1}}
:

\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E)\leq C_{+}\text{diam}(E)^{n}
, with
C_{+}>0
constant.

By the translation invariance and
\widetilde{\gamma}(\lambda E)=\lambda^{n}\widetilde{\gamma}(E)
,
\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(\lambda E)=\lambda^{n}\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E)
(see
[
He
, Proposition 3.1]
), it is clear that we may assume
E\subset B_{1}(0)
without loss of generality. In fact, in order to apply Theorem
4.1
let us check that we can assume:

\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
:

E

is contained in the unit ball and consists of a finite union of closed dyadic cubes belonging to a dyadic grid in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}

(
with sides parallel to the coordinate axes
\,)
, all with the same size and with disjoint interiors.

Let us verify that if we deduce the comparability between
\widetilde{\gamma}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}
for
E
satisfying
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
, we obtain the same result for a general
E
. So fix
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
any compact set and let
\overline{\pazocal{U}_{\delta}(E)}
be the closed
\delta
-neighborhood of
E
. Consider a grid of dyadic cubes in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with sides parallel to the axes and diameter smaller than
\delta/2
. Name
E_{0}
the collection of dyadic cubes that intersect
E
and notice that
E\subset E_{0}\subset\overline{E_{0}}\subset\overline{\pazocal{U}_{\delta}(E)}
. Now we would have

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\leq\widetilde{\gamma}(\overline{E_{0}})\lesssim\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(\overline{E_{0}})\leq\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(\overline{\pazocal{U}_{\delta}(E)})\leq\gamma_{+}(\overline{\pazocal{U}_{\delta}(E)}).

Letting
\delta\to 0
and using the outer regularity of
\gamma_{+}
we deduce, by Theorem
3.5
,

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\lesssim\gamma_{+}(E)\lesssim\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E),

and the result follows.

Finally, we present a last assumption that motivates the title of this section:

\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
.

Let

\{\pazocal{Q}_{1},\ldots,\pazocal{Q}_{N}\}

be the family of cubes provided by Theorem

4.1

and

C_{1}

the constant appearing in point
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
of the same theorem. We will assume that

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\geq C_{1}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\gamma}(2\pazocal{Q}_{i}\cap E).

The aim of this section is to prove the comparability between
\widetilde{\gamma}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}
under this last assumption
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
.

5.1.
Basic definitions and properties

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be a compact set verifying
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{1}},\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
and
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
. We begin our argument by fixing a distribution
T_{0}
admissible for
\widetilde{\gamma}(E)
so that
|\langle T_{0},1\rangle|=\widetilde{\gamma}(E)/2
. We know that
T_{0}
has
n
-growth with constant 1 and

\|P\ast T_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq 1,\qquad\|P^{\ast}\ast T_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq 1.

Let
\pazocal{F}:=\cup_{i=1}^{N}\pazocal{Q}_{i}
be the covering by cubes provided by Theorem
4.1
. Recall that
\pazocal{Q}_{i}=2Q_{i}
, where
\{Q_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
is the finite subset of dyadic cubes with disjoint interiors of a Whitney decomposition of
\pazocal{G}\supset E
, with the property
\frac{5}{4}Q_{i}\cap E\neq\varnothing
. We write

F:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}Q_{i}\subset\pazocal{F}.

It is clear that
E\subset F
and moreover, by monotonicity and the properties in Theorem
4.1
we also have

\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\prime}
.

\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(F)\leq C_{0}\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E)
,

\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}^{\prime}
.

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\leq C_{1}\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E)
and by
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
we also have
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\leq C_{1}\widetilde{\gamma}(E)
.

\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}^{\prime}
.

\text{{diam}}(Q_{i})\leq\frac{1}{20}\text{{diam}}(E)
, for each
i=1,\ldots,N
,

\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{5}}^{\prime}
.

The family
\{10Q_{1},\ldots,10Q_{N}\}
has bounded overlap. Moreover, if
10Q_{j}\cap 10Q_{i}\neq\varnothing
, then
\ell(Q_{j})\approx\ell(Q_{i})
.

In order to simplify the arguments, in this section we will work with the cubes
Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{N}
instead of
\pazocal{Q}_{1},\ldots,\pazocal{Q}_{N}
, and with
F
instead of
\pazocal{F}
. Let us choose for each
Q_{i}
a ball
B_{i}\subset\frac{1}{2}Q_{i}
concentric with
Q_{i}
with radius
r_{i}
comparable to
\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)^{1/n}
. Notice that

\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)^{1/n}\leq\gamma(2Q_{i})^{1/n}\approx\ell(Q_{i}).

Therefore, we may choose
r_{i}\approx\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)^{1/n}
with constants depending at most on the dimension and still have
B_{i}\subset\frac{1}{2}Q_{i}
. Notice that
\text{dist}(B_{i},B_{j})\geq\frac{1}{2}\min\{\ell(Q_{i}),\ell(Q_{j})\}
, for
i\neq j
. We define the positive measure

(5.1)

\mu:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mu_{i}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{r_{i}^{n}}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{B_{i}}.

Apply
[
HPo
, Lemma 3.1]
to pick test functions
\varphi_{i}\in\pazocal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(2Q_{i}),0\leq\varphi_{i}\leq 1
, satisfying
\|\nabla\varphi_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\ell(2Q_{i})^{-1}
and with
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varphi_{i}\equiv 1
in
F
. We also define the signed measure

(5.2)

\nu:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{B_{i}},\qquad\text{that is}\qquad\nu=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle}{r_{i}^{n}}\mu_{i}.

Since
\text{supp}(\nu)\subset\text{supp}(\mu)\subset\pazocal{F}
and
\nu\ll\mu
, have
\nu=b\mu
, where
b
is the Radon-Nikodym derivative.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following hold for some positive constants
c_{1},c_{3}
and
c_{4}
:

1
.

\|b\|_{\infty}\leq c_{1}
.

2
.

|\nu(F)|=\widetilde{\gamma}(E)/2
.

3
.

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\leq c_{3}\mu(F)
.

4.

|\nu(Q)|\leq c_{4}\ell(Q)^{n}
for any cube
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.

Proof.

Let us prove
1
. By the localization result
[
MPr
, Theorem 3.1]
we get
\|P\ast(\varphi_{i}T_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq A_{1}
, for every
i
, where
A_{1}=A_{1}(n)>0
. Analogously, the same localization result holds for
P^{\ast}
, so there also exists
A_{2}=A_{2}(n)>0
with
\|P^{\ast}\ast(\varphi_{i}T_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq A_{2}
,
\forall i
. Therefore, since
\text{supp}(\varphi_{i}T_{0})\subset 2Q_{i}\cap E
we have

(5.3)

|\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle|=|\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle|\lesssim\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\approx r_{i}^{n},\quad\forall i=1,\ldots,n,

which yields the desired result.

To prove
2
we simply use the definition of
\nu
,
B_{i}\cap F=B_{i}
and
\text{supp}(T_{0})\subset E\subset F
:

\displaystyle|\nu(F)|=\Bigg\rvert\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\int_{B_{i}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}\Bigg\rvert=\Bigg\rvert\Bigg\langle T_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varphi_{i}\Bigg\rangle\Bigg\rvert=|\langle T_{0},1\rangle|=\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\gamma}(E).

Finally,
3
follows by the choice of radii
r_{i}
and the admissibility of
\varphi_{i}T_{0}
for
\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}(E)=2|\langle T_{0},1\rangle|\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\approx\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{i}^{n}=\mu(F).

The proof of inequality
4
can be followed analogously to that given for property
(g)
of
[
To3
, Lemma 6.8]
. We refer the reader to this last reference for the detailed arguments.
∎

Remark
5.1
.

In the proof of inequality
4
it is used that for a Borel signed measure
\nu
satisfying
\|P\ast\nu\|_{\infty}\leq 1
one has
|\nu(Q)|\leq C\ell(Q)^{n}
, for some constant
C>0
and any cube
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. In order to justify that this holds in our context we will prove the following: given
\varphi\in\pazocal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
such that
\|P\ast\varphi\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, then
|\int_{Q}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|\leq C\ell(Q)^{n}
, for some
C>0
constant. If this is the case, we will be done by mollifying
\nu
to be
\nu_{\varepsilon}:=\nu\ast\psi_{\varepsilon}
with
(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
a proper approximation of the identity. Let us estimate
|\int_{Q}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|
as follows:

\displaystyle\bigg\rvert\int_{Q}\varphi(\overline{x})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{x}\bigg\rvert\leq\bigg\rvert\int_{Q}\partial_{t}(P\ast\varphi)(\overline{x})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{x}\bigg\rvert+\bigg\rvert\int_{Q}(\Delta_{x})^{1/2}(P\ast\varphi)(\overline{x})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{x}\bigg\rvert

For the first term of the right-hand side we set
Q=Q_{1}\times I_{Q}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
where
I_{Q}
is the
temporal
interval, that we write as
I_{Q}=[a,b]
for some
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
with
a<b
. This way, by Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\bigg\rvert\int_{Q}\partial_{t}(P\ast\varphi)(\overline{x})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{x}\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg\rvert\int_{Q_{1}}\Big(P\ast\varphi(x,b)-P\ast\varphi(x,a)\Big)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\bigg\rvert\lesssim\|P\ast\varphi\|_{\infty}\pazocal{L}^{n}(Q_{1})\leq\ell(Q)^{n}.

For the second term we recall that we have the following representation

(-\Delta)^{1/2}\approx\sum_{j=1}^{n}\partial_{j}R_{j}

where
R_{j},\,1\leq j\leq n
are the Riesz transforms with Fourier multiplier
\xi_{j}/|\xi_{j}|
. Notice that if we prove that
P\ast(R_{j}\varphi)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
for each
j=1,\ldots,n
, we are done, since we may argue as above decomposing the cube
Q
as a cartesian product of an interval and an
n
-dimensional cube. To study
P\ast(R_{j}\varphi)
we consider its Fourier transform
\frac{\xi_{j}}{|\xi|}\widehat{P}\widehat{\varphi}
. Notice that
P\in(L^{1}+L^{2})(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
(just decompose it as
P\chi_{|\overline{x}|\leq 1}+P\chi_{|\overline{x}|>1}
), and therefore
\widehat{P}\in(L^{\infty}+L^{2})(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
, and we write its decomposition as
\widehat{P}_{\infty}+\widehat{P}_{2}
. Now it is immediate to check that
\frac{\xi_{j}}{|\xi|}\widehat{P}_{\infty}\widehat{\varphi}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
and we also have
\frac{\xi_{j}}{|\xi|}\widehat{P}_{2}\widehat{\varphi}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Therefore the Fourier transform of
P\ast(R_{j}\varphi)
is integrable, implying
P\ast(R_{j}\varphi)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
that is what we wanted to see.

5.2.
The exceptional sets
\boldsymbol{H_{\pazocal{D}}}
and
\boldsymbol{T_{\pazocal{D}}}

In light of the third property in Lemma
5.1
, assume we find a compact set
G\subset F
such that

i
.

\mu(F)\lesssim\mu(G)
,

ii
.

\mu|_{G}
has
n
-growth,

iii
.

\pazocal{P}_{\mu|_{G}}
is a bounded operator in
L^{2}(\mu|_{G})
.

Then,
\mu|_{G}
is admissible for
\gamma_{\text{op}}(G)
, and we are done, since we have

(5.4)

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\lesssim\mu(F)\lesssim\mu(G)\leq\gamma_{\text{op}}(G)\leq\gamma_{\text{op}}(F)\approx\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(F)\lesssim\widetilde{\gamma}_{+}(E),

where the first inequality is due to Lemma
5.1
and the last is by property
\text{{{P}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\prime}
above.

The construction of
G
is inspired by the
Tb
-theorems found in
[
To3
, Theorem 5.1]
and
[
Vo
, Theorem 7.1]
, which are presented for the particular case of Cauchy and Riesz potentials respectively. To apply this kind of results we need to introduce the notions of Ahlfors ball, Ahlfors radius and Ahlfors point in our context. Due to
[
MPr
, Lemma 4.1]
there are positive dimensional constants
a_{1},a_{2}
such that

\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\leq a_{1}\,\text{diam}(E)^{n},

and also

a_{2}^{-1}r_{i}^{n}\leq\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\leq a_{2}r_{i}^{n},

for each
i=1,\ldots,N
, where
r_{i}
is the radius of the ball
B_{i}\subset Q_{i}
constructed at the beginning of Subsection
5.1
. Let

L:=100^{n}a_{1}a_{2}C_{1},

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are the constants appearing in Theorem
4.1
. The factor 100 is arbitrarily chosen; we may pick, for convenience, any other
large
constant. Assumption
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
implies

\displaystyle\mu(F)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{i}^{n}\leq a_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\leq a_{2}C_{1}\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\leq a_{1}a_{2}C_{1}\,\text{diam}(E)^{n}.

Therefore:

\forall\overline{x}\in F,\,\forall R>\frac{1}{100}\text{diam}(F)\,:\quad\mu(B_{R}(\overline{x}))\leq LR^{n}.

A ball
B_{R}(\overline{x})
,
\overline{x}\in F
, is an
Ahlfors ball
if precisely the estimate
\mu(B_{R}(\overline{x}))\leq LR^{n}
holds. Notice that if
R>\frac{1}{100}\text{diam}(F)
, then the ball is an Ahlfors ball.
We also define the
Ahlfors radius
,

(5.5)

\pazocal{R}(\overline{x}):=\sup\big\{r>0:B_{r}(\overline{x})\text{ is non-Ahlfors}\,\big\}.

Ahlfors points
are those for which
\pazocal{R}(\overline{x})=0
. For every
\overline{x}\in F
, we have
\pazocal{R}(\overline{x})\leq\frac{1}{100}\text{diam}(F)
. Set

H^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{\overline{x}\in F}B_{\pazocal{R}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x}),

where we convey
B_{0}(\overline{x}):=\varnothing
, and apply a
5r
-covering theorem
[
Ma
, Theorem 2.1]
to choose a countable family of disjoint balls
\{B_{\pazocal{R}_{k}}\}_{k}:=\{B_{\pazocal{R}(\overline{x}_{k})}(\overline{x}_{k})\}_{k}
such that

(5.6)

H^{\prime}\subset H:=\bigcup_{k}B_{5\pazocal{R}_{k}},

so that all non-Ahlfors balls are contained in
H
. Observe that since
\mu(B_{\pazocal{R}_{k}})\geq L\pazocal{R}_{k}^{n}
and the balls
B_{\pazocal{R}_{k}}
are disjoint, we have

\sum_{k}\pazocal{R}_{k}^{n}\leq\frac{1}{L}\sum_{k}\mu(B_{\pazocal{R}_{k}})\leq\frac{1}{L}\mu(F).

Let
\pazocal{D}^{0}
be the usual dyadic lattice in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}

and for
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
we write

\pazocal{D}(\overline{x}):=\overline{x}+\pazocal{D}^{0},

the translation of
\pazocal{D}^{0}
by the vector
\overline{x}
. Let
\pazocal{D}=\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})
be any fixed dyadic lattice from the family of lattices
\{\pazocal{D}(\overline{y})\}_{\overline{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}
. Consider the subcollection of dyadic cubes
\pazocal{D}_{H}\subset\pazocal{D}
defined as follows:
Q\in\pazocal{D}_{H}
if there is a ball
B_{5\pazocal{R}_{k}}(\overline{x}_{k})
satisfying

(5.7)

B_{5\pazocal{R}_{k}}(\overline{x}_{k})\cap Q\neq\varnothing\qquad\text{and}\qquad 10\pazocal{R}_{k}<\ell(Q)\leq 20\pazocal{R}_{k}.

Then, it is clear that

\bigcup_{k}B_{5\pazocal{R}_{k}}(\overline{x}_{k})\subset\bigcup_{Q\in\pazocal{D}_{H}}Q.

Pick a subfamily of disjoint maximal cubes
\{Q_{k}\}_{k}
from
\pazocal{D}_{H}
so that we may define the exceptional set
H{D}
as follows

\bigcup_{Q\in\pazocal{D}_{H}}Q=\bigcup_{k}Q_{k}=:H_{\pazocal{D}}=H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}.

By construction one has

H\subset H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})},\quad\forall\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1},

and since for each ball
B_{5\pazocal{R}_{k}}(\overline{x}_{k})
conditions (
5.7
) can only occur for a number of cubes in
\pazocal{D}_{H}
which is bounded by a dimensional constant, there is
c_{H}=c_{H}(n)>0
so that

(5.8)

\sum_{k}\ell(Q_{k})^{n}\leq c_{H}\sum_{k}\pazocal{R}_{k}^{n}\leq\frac{c_{H}}{L}\mu(F).

We define the exceptional set
T{D}
in a similar manner but imposing a proper
accretivity condition
on the Radon-Nikodym derivative
b
. Consider the family
\pazocal{D}_{T}\subset\pazocal{D}
of cubes satisfying

(5.9)

\mu(Q)\geq c_{T}|\nu(Q)|,

with
c_{T}=c_{T}(n)>0
big constant to be fixed below. Let
\{Q_{k}\}_{k}
be the subcollection of maximal (and thus disjoint) dyadic cubes from
\pazocal{D}_{T}
. We define the exceptional set
T{D}=T_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}
as

T{D}:=\bigcup_{k}Q_{k}.

The next result shows that
\mu(F\setminus{(H_{\pazocal{D}}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}})})
is comparable to
\mu(F)
. The reader can find its proof in
[
To3
, Lemma 6.12]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

If
L
and
c_{T}
are chosen big enough, then

|\nu(H_{\pazocal{D}}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}})|\leq\frac{1}{2}|\nu(F)|\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mu(H_{\pazocal{D}}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}})\leq\delta_{0}\mu(F),

with
\delta_{0}<1
constant.

5.3.
Verifying the hypotheses of a
Tb\,
-theorem

The goal of this subsection is to prove the lemma below, analogous to
[
To3
, Lemma 6.8 (Main Lemma)]
. We remark that in order to apply the
Tb
-theorem found in
[
NTVo1
]
we will still need to check an additional weak boundedness property for a particular suppressed kernel, due to the lack of anti-symmetry of our kernel
P
. In the proof of the aforementioned reference or that of
[
To3
, Theorem 5.1]
, such condition needs not to be verified, by the anti-symmetry of the Cauchy and Riesz kernels.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be a compact set verifying
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{1}},\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
and
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
. Let
\pazocal{F}:=\cup_{i=1}^{N}\pazocal{Q}_{i}
be the covering provided by Theorem
4.1
, and
F:=\cup_{i=1}^{N}Q_{i}=\cup_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{2}\pazocal{Q}_{i}
, that still satisfies
E\subset F
. Let
\mu
and
\nu
be the measures defined in (
5.1
) and (
5.2
). Then, there exists a subset
H\subset F
, such that

1
.

\nu=b\mu
for some
b
with
\|b\|_{\infty}\leq c_{1}
.

2
.

|\nu(F)|=\widetilde{\gamma}(E)/2
.

3
.

c_{3}^{-1}\widetilde{\gamma}(E)\leq\mu(F)\leq c_{3}\widetilde{\gamma}(E)
.

4
.

For any cube
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,
|\nu(Q)|\leq c_{4}\ell(Q)^{n}
.

5
.

If
\pazocal{P}_{\ast}
and
\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\ast}
denote the maximal convolution operators associated to
P
and
P^{\ast}
respectively, we have

\int_{F\setminus{H}}\pazocal{P}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{x})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{x})\leq c_{5}\,\mu(F),\qquad\int_{F\setminus{H}}\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{x})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{x})\leq c_{5}\,\mu(F).

6
.

If
\mu(B_{r}(\overline{x}))>Lr^{n}

(\,
for some big constant
L)
, then
B_{r}(\overline{x})\subset H
. In particular, for any
\overline{x}\in F\setminus{H}
and
r>0
, one has
\mu(B_{r}(\overline{x}))\leq Lr^{n}
.

7
.

H
is of the form
\bigcup_{k\in I_{H}}B_{r_{k}}(\overline{x}_{k})
, for some countable set of indices
I_{H}
. Moreover
\sum_{k\in I_{H}}r_{k}^{n}\leq\frac{5^{n}}{L}\mu(F)
.

The constants
c_{1},c_{3},c_{4}
and
c_{5}
are fixed, while
L
can be chosen arbitrarily large.

Many of the hypothesis in Lemma
5.3
are verified for our particular choice of
\mu
and
\nu
. Properties
1
,
2
and
4
are proved in Lemma
5.1
, and
3
follows from the third property of the same lemma, and by assumption
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}
combined with
r_{i}^{n}\approx\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)
. Moreover, taking
H\subset F
to be as in (
5.6
), properties
6
and
7
are also guaranteed by definition. So it remains to check hypothesis
5
. To do so, let us first present a series of auxiliary results.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
T
be a distribution supported on a compact set
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with
\|P\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
or
\|P^{\ast}\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Let
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be a cube and
\varphi\in\pazocal{C}^{1}_{c,\pazocal{N}}(2Q)
such that
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\leq D\,\ell(Q)
and
\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\infty}\leq A\,D
. Then

|\langle T,\varphi\rangle|\lesssim D\,\ell(Q)^{n+1}.

Proof.

Recall Remark
2.1
to notice that the proof is simply an application of
[
MPr
, Corollary 3.3]
to
\psi:=\big[2AD\ell(Q)\big]^{-1}\varphi\in\pazocal{C}^{1}_{c,\pazocal{N}}(2Q)
that is such that
\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\leq\ell(2Q)^{-1}
. Observe that
[
MPr
, Corollary 3.3]
is also valid for
P^{\ast}
, which can be proved analogously using that
P^{\ast}
is fundamental solution of the conjugate operator
\overline{\Theta}^{1/2}
.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be compact set and
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
a cube. If
T
is a distribution supported on
E\subset Q
, then the following holds:

1
.

If
\|P\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
or
\|P^{\ast}\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\varphi\in\pazocal{C}^{1}_{\pazocal{N}}(2Q)
satisfies
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(2Q)}\leq D\,\ell(Q)
and
\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(2Q)}\leq D
, then

|\langle T,\varphi\rangle|\lesssim D\,\ell(Q)^{n+1}.

2
.

If
\|P\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
,
\|P^{\ast}\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\varphi
is as above, then

|\langle T,\varphi\rangle|\lesssim D\,\ell(Q)\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}(E).

Proof.

We prove
1
under the assumption
\|P\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
(the proof is analogous for
P^{\ast}
). Consider a test function
\psi\in\pazocal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(2Q)
with
\|\psi\|_{\infty}\leq 1,\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\leq\ell(2Q)^{-1}
and
\psi|_{Q}\equiv 1
. Notice that
\eta:=\varphi\psi
belongs to
\pazocal{C}^{1}_{c,\pazocal{N}}(2Q)
and
\|\eta\|_{\infty}\leq D\,\ell(Q)
and
\|\nabla\eta\|_{\infty}\leq 2D
. Then, by Lemma
5.4
and the fact that
\text{supp}(T)\subset E\subset Q
,

|\langle T,\varphi\rangle|=|\langle T,\eta\rangle|\lesssim D\,\ell(Q)^{n+1},

To prove
2
, consider
\psi
as before and define

\eta:=\big[4D\ell(Q)\big]^{-1}\varphi\psi\in\pazocal{C}^{1}_{c,\pazocal{N}}(2Q),

that is such that
\|\nabla\eta\|_{\infty}\leq\ell(2Q)^{-1}
. By Remark
2.1
, we shall apply
[
MPr
, Theorem 3.1]
to deduce
\|P\ast\eta T\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
and
\|P^{\ast}\ast\eta T\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
. Hence
|\langle\eta T,1\rangle|\lesssim\widetilde{\gamma}(E)
, meaning

|\langle T,\varphi\rangle|\lesssim D\,\ell(Q)\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}(E).

∎

Let us now consider a distribution
T
supported on
E\subset Q=Q(c_{Q},\ell(Q))
with
\|P\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\|P^{\ast}\ast T\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Fix any
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{3Q}
and set

\varphi_{\overline{z}}(\cdot):=P(\overline{z}-\cdot)-P(\overline{z}-c_{Q}),\qquad\varphi^{\ast}_{\overline{z}}(\cdot):=P^{\ast}(\overline{z}-\cdot)-P^{\ast}(\overline{z}-c_{Q}).

Write
\overline{z}=(z,\tau)
and let
\overline{y}=(y,s)
be a generic point in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. We define the null set
\pazocal{N}=\pazocal{N}(\overline{z})
to be the horizontal hyperplane

\pazocal{N}:=\big\{\overline{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\,:\,s=\tau\big\}.

This way
\varphi_{\overline{z}},\varphi_{\overline{z}}^{\ast}\in\pazocal{C}^{\infty}_{\pazocal{N}}(2Q)
. Moreover, for any
\overline{y}\in 2Q
, by
[
MPr
, Lemma 2.1]
,

|\varphi_{\overline{z}}(\overline{y})|=|P(\overline{z}-\overline{y})-P(\overline{z}-c_{Q})|\lesssim\frac{|\overline{y}-c_{Q}|}{|\overline{z}-c_{Q}|^{n+1}}\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}},

and for any
\overline{y}\in 2Q\setminus{\pazocal{N}}
,

|\nabla\varphi_{\overline{z}}(\overline{y})|=|\nabla P(\overline{z}-\overline{y})|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\overline{z}-\overline{y}|^{n+1}}\leq\frac{3^{n+1}}{|\overline{z}-c_{Q}|}\lesssim\frac{1}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}}.

and the same bounds clearly also hold for
\varphi_{\overline{z}}^{\ast}
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\overline{z}}\|_{L^{\infty}(2Q)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}},\qquad\|\varphi_{\overline{z}}^{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(2Q)}\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla\varphi_{\overline{z}}\|_{L^{\infty}(2Q)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}},\qquad\|\nabla\varphi_{\overline{z}}^{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(2Q)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}}.

Hence, using the identities

\displaystyle\big\rvert P\ast T(\overline{z})-\langle T,1\rangle P(\overline{z}-c_{Q})\big\rvert

\displaystyle=|\langle T,\varphi_{\overline{z}}\rangle|,

\displaystyle\big\rvert P^{\ast}\ast T(\overline{z})-\langle T,1\rangle P^{\ast}(\overline{z}-c_{Q})\big\rvert

\displaystyle=|\langle T,\varphi_{\overline{z}}^{\ast}\rangle|,

a direct application of the second statement in Lemma
5.5
yields

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be a compact set contained in the cube
Q=Q(c_{Q},\ell(Q))
, and
T
a distribution admissible for
\widetilde{\gamma}(E)
. Then, for any
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{3Q}
,

\displaystyle\big\rvert P\ast T(\overline{z})-\langle T,1\rangle P(\overline{z}-c_{Q})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\text{{dist}}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(E),

\displaystyle\big\rvert P^{\ast}\ast T(\overline{z})-\langle T,1\rangle P^{\ast}(\overline{z}-c_{Q})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\text{{dist}}(\overline{z},E)^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(E).

Lemma 5.7
.

Let

\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}:=\frac{\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\pazocal{L}^{n+1}|_{B_{i}},\quad i=1,\ldots,N.

For each
i=1,\ldots,N
, we have

1
.

\|P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
and
\|P\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
.

2
.

For any
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{3Q_{i}}
,

\big\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-P\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}(\overline{z})\big\rvert\lesssim\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{{dist}}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E).

The same results hold changing
P
by
P^{\ast}
.

Proof.

The first estimate in
1
is just a consequence of the localization result
[
MPr
, Theorem 3.1]
. Regarding the second, fix
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and compute:

\displaystyle|P\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}|(\overline{x})\leq\frac{|\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle|}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\int_{B_{i}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n}}\lesssim\frac{1}{r_{i}}\int_{B_{i}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n}},

where we have used that
|\langle T_{0},\varphi_{i}\rangle|\lesssim r_{i}^{n}
, that has already been argued in (
5.3
). We deal with remaining integral as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{B_{i}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n}}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{i}\cap\{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|\geq r_{i}\}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n}}+\int_{B_{i}\cap\{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|<r_{i}\}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}{r_{i}^{n}}+\int_{B_{4r_{i}}(\overline{x})}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{x}-\overline{y}|^{n}}\lesssim r_{i}.

Let us prove
2
: fix
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{3Q_{i}}
and notice

\big\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-P\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}(\overline{z})\big\rvert=\bigg\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-\frac{\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle}{r_{i}^{n}}P\ast\mu_{i}(\overline{z})\bigg\rvert,

where

\frac{1}{r_{i}^{n}}P\ast\mu_{i}(\overline{z})=\int_{B_{i}}\big(P(\overline{z}-\overline{y})-P(\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}})\big)\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{i}(\overline{y})}{r_{i}^{n}}+P(\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\big\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})

\displaystyle-P\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}(\overline{z})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\big\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle P(\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}})\big\rvert

(5.10)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{|\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle|}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\int_{B_{i}}\big\rvert P(\overline{z}-\overline{y})-P(\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}.

Apply Corollary
5.6
to
T:=\varphi_{i}T_{0}
, admissible for
\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)
to deduce

\displaystyle\big\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle P(\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i}\cap E)^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E).

By
[
MPr
, Lemma 2.1]
and estimates in the proof of Lemma
5.1
we also have

\displaystyle\frac{|\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle|}{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}\int_{B_{i}}\big\rvert P(\overline{z}-\overline{y})-

\displaystyle P(\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{r_{i}}\frac{1}{|\overline{z}-c_{B_{i}}|^{n+1}}\int_{B_{i}}|\overline{y}-c_{B_{i}}|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\pazocal{L}^{n+1}(B_{i})}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\approx\frac{r_{i}}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2\pazocal{Q}_{i}\cap E)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E).

Hence, using (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) in (
5.10
) we finish the proof of
2
.
∎

Our next goal will be to obtain a regularized version of statement
2
in Lemma
5.7
. Consider
\psi
a smooth radial function
\psi
supported on
B_{1}(0)
with
0\leq\psi\leq 1
,
\int\psi=1
and
\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, and set

\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x}):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n+1}}\psi\bigg(\frac{\overline{x}}{\varepsilon}\bigg),\quad\varepsilon>0.

Notice that
\int\Psi_{\varepsilon}=1
for every
\varepsilon>0
. Define the regularized kernels

R_{\varepsilon}:=\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast P,\qquad R_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}:=\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast P^{\ast},

as well as
\pazocal{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}
and
\pazocal{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, its associated convolution operators with respect to the finite Borel measure
\mu
.

Remark
5.2
.

Observe that, in particular, for a point
\overline{x}_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})
with
|\overline{x}_{0}|>\varepsilon
and
t_{0}\neq 0
we have

R_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x}_{0})=P(\overline{x}_{0})\quad\text{ and }\quad R_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}(\overline{x}_{0})=P^{\ast}(\overline{x}_{0}).

This follows as in
[
Vo
, Equation 3.32]
, using the harmonicity of
P
and
P^{\ast}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{\{t=0\}}
and the mean value property in that domain. Notice that we need the spherical symmetry of
\Psi_{\varepsilon}
and
\int\Psi_{\varepsilon}=1
to ensure this.

On the other hand, for any
\overline{x}_{0}
such that
|\overline{x}_{0}|<\varepsilon
we have

|R_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x}_{0})|\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n}}\quad\text{ and }\quad|R_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}(\overline{x}_{0})|\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n}}.

Indeed, using the definition of
\Psi
and the fact that
0\leq\psi\leq 1
, we get

|R_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x}_{0})|\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n+1}}\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{x}}{|\overline{x}_{0}-\overline{x}|^{n}}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n+1}}\int_{2B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x}_{0})}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{x}}{|\overline{x}_{0}-\overline{x}|^{n}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n}},

and analogously for
R_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}(\overline{x}_{0})
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{4Q_{i}}
, any
\varepsilon>0
and each
i=1,\ldots,N
,

\big\rvert R_{\varepsilon}\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-R_{\varepsilon}\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}(\overline{z})\big\rvert\lesssim\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{{dist}}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E).

The same result holds changing
R_{\varepsilon}
by
R_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
.

Proof.

We will proof the result only for
R_{\varepsilon}
, since the arguments for
R_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
are analogous. Fix
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{4Q_{i}}
and
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})
. This way, since for any
\overline{y}\in B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z})
we have
\text{dist}(\overline{y},2Q_{i})\approx\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})
, by Lemma
5.7
and the fact that
\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z})}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z}-\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}=1
we deduce

\displaystyle\big\rvert R_{\varepsilon}\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{z})-R_{\varepsilon}\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}(\overline{z})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z})}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z}-\overline{y})\big\rvert P\ast\varphi_{i}T_{0}(\overline{y})-P\ast\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}(\overline{y})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{{dist}}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E),

the desired inequality. Hence, we are only left to study the case
\varepsilon\geq\frac{1}{2}\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})
. Observe that in this setting, since
\overline{z}\not\in 4Q_{i}
we have
\varepsilon\gtrsim\ell(Q_{i})
. Write

\alpha_{i}:=\varphi_{i}T_{0}-\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}},

so that

\displaystyle|R_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}(\overline{z})|\leq\int_{\text{supp}(\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i})}P(\overline{z}-\overline{y})\big\rvert\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}(\overline{y})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}.

Observe that
\text{supp}(\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}})\subset B_{i},\,\text{supp}(\varphi_{i}T_{0})\subset 2Q_{i}\cap E
and
\text{supp}(\Psi_{\varepsilon})\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
. Then, the support of
\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}
is contained in
\pazocal{U}_{\varepsilon}(2Q_{i})
, an open
\varepsilon
-neighborhood of
2Q_{i}
. This implies

\displaystyle|R_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}(\overline{z})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}\|_{\infty}\int_{\pazocal{U}_{\varepsilon}(2Q_{i})}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\overline{y}}{|\overline{z}-\overline{y}|^{n}}\lesssim(\ell(Q_{i})+\varepsilon)\cdot\|\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\cdot\|\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}\|_{\infty},

by integrating over decreasing annuli centered at
\overline{z}
, and using that
\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})\leq 2\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon\gtrsim\ell(Q_{i})
. On the other hand, if
\eta_{i}\in\pazocal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(3Q_{i})
with
0\leq\eta_{i}\leq 1
,
\eta_{i}\equiv 1
on
2Q_{i}
(where the support of
\alpha_{i}
is contained) and
\|\nabla\eta_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\ell(Q_{i})^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}(\overline{w})

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-\cdot),\alpha_{i}\rangle=\langle\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-\cdot)-\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-c_{Q_{i}}),\alpha_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle\big(\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-\cdot)-\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-c_{Q_{i}})\big)\eta_{i},\alpha_{i}\big\rangle,

where we have used
\langle\varphi_{i}T_{0},1\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}},1\rangle
.

We claim that for each
\overline{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, the function

\varphi(\overline{\xi}):=\big(\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-\xi)-\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-c_{Q_{i}})\big)\eta_{i}(\xi),

satisfies
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-(n+2)}\ell(Q_{i})
and
\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\infty}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-(n+2)}
. Then, using that statement
1
in Lemma
5.7
implies that
\varphi T_{0}
is admissible for
\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)
and that
\widehat{\varphi T_{0}}
is admissible for
\widetilde{\gamma}(B_{i})
, we have, by Lemma
5.5
,

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}|=|\langle\varphi,\alpha_{i}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\varepsilon^{n+2}}\big\{\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)+\widetilde{\gamma}(B_{i})\big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\varepsilon^{n+2}}\big\{\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)+r_{i}^{n}\big\}\approx\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\varepsilon^{n+2}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E).

Therefore,

\displaystyle|R_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}(\overline{z})|\lesssim\varepsilon\cdot\|\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\alpha_{i}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\varepsilon^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\lesssim\frac{\ell(2Q_{i})}{\text{{dist}}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E),

where we have used
\text{dist}(\overline{z},2Q_{i})\leq 2\varepsilon
, and we deduce the desired estimate. Hence, we are left to prove the claim. Let us fix
\overline{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and compute: on the one hand

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{\infty}=\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(3Q_{i})}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n+2}}\sup_{\overline{\xi}\in 3Q_{i}}|\overline{\xi}-c_{Q_{i}}|\cdot\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\varepsilon^{n+2}},

while on the other hand

\displaystyle\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(3Q_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\ell(Q_{i})}\sup_{\overline{\xi}\in 3Q_{i}}\big\rvert\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-\overline{\xi})-\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}-c_{Q_{i}})\big\rvert+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n+2}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n+2}}.

Hence, we are done.
∎

Let us finally prove property
5
in Lemma
5.3
:

Lemma 5.9
.

The following estimates hold:

\int_{F\setminus{H}}\pazocal{P}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})\lesssim\mu(F),\qquad\int_{F\setminus{H}}\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})\lesssim\mu(F).

Proof.

We only prove the first estimate, since the proof of the second is analogous. Begin by noticing that

\displaystyle\int_{F\setminus{H}}\pazocal{P}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{y})

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})=\int_{F\setminus{H}}\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{F\setminus{H}}\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{y})-\pazocal{R}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})+\int_{F\setminus{H}}\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\big\rvert\pazocal{R}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
, observe that for each
\varepsilon>0
and
\overline{y}=(y,s)\in F\setminus{H}
, since
\nu\ll\mu\ll\pazocal{L}^{n+1}
, we have

\nu\big(\{(z,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\,:\,\tau=s\}\big)=0,

so applying Remark
5.2
we deduce

\displaystyle\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon}\nu

\displaystyle(\overline{y})-\pazocal{R}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert

\displaystyle=\Bigg\rvert\int_{|\overline{y}-\overline{z}|\leq\varepsilon}R_{\varepsilon}(\overline{y}-\overline{z})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\overline{z})+\int_{|\overline{y}-\overline{z}|>\varepsilon}R_{\varepsilon}(\overline{y}-\overline{z})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\overline{z})-\int_{|\overline{y}-\overline{z}|>\varepsilon}P(\overline{y}-\overline{z})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\overline{z})\Bigg\rvert

\displaystyle=\Bigg\rvert\int_{|\overline{y}-\overline{z}|\leq\varepsilon}R_{\varepsilon}(\overline{y}-\overline{z})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\overline{z})\Bigg\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n}}|\nu|(B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{y}))\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n}}\mu(B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{y}))\leq M\mu(\overline{y}).

Then, using that
\overline{y}\in F\setminus{H}
we get
|I_{1}|\lesssim\mu(F)
. So we are left to estimate
I_{2}
. We introduce the notation

R_{\ast}\ast\nu(\overline{y}):=\sup_{\varepsilon>0}|R_{\varepsilon}\ast\nu(\overline{y})|,

so that

\displaystyle I_{2}:=\int_{F\setminus{H}}R_{\ast}\ast\nu(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})\leq\int_{F\setminus{H}}R_{\ast}\ast T_{0}(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})+\int_{F\setminus{H}}R_{\ast}\ast(\nu-T_{0})(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y}).

Since
\|P\ast T_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
by construction, we also have
\|R_{\varepsilon}\ast T_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, uniformly on
\varepsilon>0
. For the second integral in
I_{2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{F\setminus{H}}

\displaystyle R_{\ast}\ast(\nu-T_{0})(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{F\setminus{H}}R_{\ast}\ast\big(\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}-\varphi_{i}T_{0}\big)(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigg(\int_{4Q_{i}}R_{\ast}\ast\big(\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}-\varphi_{i}T_{0}\big)(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})+\int_{F\setminus{(4Q_{i}\cup H)}}R_{\ast}\ast\big(\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}-\varphi_{i}T_{0}\big)(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt=:\sum_{i=1}^{N}I_{i,1}+I_{i,2},

where in the first inequality we have used that
\sum_{i}\varphi_{i}\equiv 1
on
F
and
\nu=\sum_{i}\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}
. For each
i=1,\ldots,N
we set
\alpha_{i}:=\widetilde{\varphi_{i}T_{0}}-\varphi_{i}T_{0}
and apply the first statement in Lemma
5.7
to deduce
\|P\ast\alpha_{i}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
. Hence,
I_{i,1}\lesssim\mu(4Q_{i})
. To estimate
I_{i,2}
we will use Lemma
5.8
. Let
N
be the smallest integer such that

A_{N}:=\big(4^{N+1}Q_{i}\setminus{4^{N}Q_{i}}\big)\setminus{H}\neq\varnothing.

Then,

\displaystyle I_{i,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell(2Q_{i})\,\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\int_{A_{N}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})}{\text{dist}(\overline{y},2Q_{i})^{n+1}}\lesssim\ell(2Q_{i})\,\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\frac{\mu\big(4^{k+1}Q_{i}\big)}{(4^{k}\ell(Q_{i}))^{n+1}}.

Observe that for any
\overline{x}\in A_{N}
, we have for each
k\geq N

\mu\big(4^{k+1}Q_{i}\big)\leq\mu\big(B_{2\cdot\text{diam}(4^{k+1}Q_{i})}(\overline{x})\big)\lesssim\big(4^{k}\ell(Q_{i})\big)^{n},

where we have used that
\overline{x}\not\in H
. Therefore,

\displaystyle I_{i,2}\lesssim\ell(2Q_{i})\,\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4^{k}\ell(Q_{i})}\lesssim\widetilde{\gamma}(2Q_{i}\cap E)\approx r_{i}^{n}=\mu(B_{i}).

Since the cubes
10Q_{1},\ldots,10Q_{N}
have bounded overlap we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{F\setminus{H}}

\displaystyle R_{\ast}\ast(\nu-T_{0})(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{y})\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mu(4Q_{i})\lesssim\mu(F).

∎

The proof of
5
concludes that of Lemma
5.3
. In order to apply the
Tb
-theorem of
[
NTVo1
]
or
[
To3
, Theorem 5.1]
we still need to check an additional weak boundedness property for our
n
-dimensional CZ kernel, which fails being anti-symmetric.

5.4.
The exceptional sets
\pazocal{S}
and
W_{\pazocal{D}}
. The additional eighth property

Assume that
F\subset B_{2^{N-3}}(0)
for some integer
N
large enough. Observe that assumption
\text{{{A}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
together with properties
1
and
4
in Theorem
4.1
imply that we can take
N=4
, for example. We write

\Omega:=\big[-2^{N-1},2^{N-1}\big)^{n+1}

and consider the
random
cube
Q^{0}(\overline{w}):=\overline{w}+[-2^{N},2^{N})^{n+1}
, with
\overline{w}\in\Omega
. Observe that
F\subset Q^{0}(\overline{w})
for any
\overline{w}\in\Omega
. Let
\mathbb{P}
be the uniform probability measure on
\Omega
, that is, the normalized Lebesgue measure on the cube
\Omega
.

When carefully reviewing the proofs of the the non-homogeneous
Tb
-theorems of
[
To3
, §5]
and
[
Vo
]
, one encounters expressions that, in our context, would be of the form

\langle\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\mu}(\chi_{\Delta}b),(\chi_{\Delta}b)\rangle:=\int_{\Delta}\int_{\Delta}P_{\Lambda}(\overline{x},\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\overline{y})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\overline{x}),

where
\Lambda
is a certain 1-Lipschitz function associated to the suppressed kernel
P_{\Lambda}
,
\Delta=P\cap S
is a parallelepiped obtained as the intersection of two cubes
P\in\pazocal{D}(\overline{w}_{1})=:\pazocal{D}_{1}
,
S\in\pazocal{D}(\overline{w}_{2})=:\pazocal{D}_{2}
, with
\overline{w}_{1},\overline{w}_{2}\in\Omega
, and
\pazocal{D}_{1}
and
\pazocal{D}_{2}
are two dyadic lattices which are reciprocally
good
to one another. Moreover, such cubes
P,S
satisfy
P\subset F\setminus{(H_{\pazocal{D}_{1}}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}_{1}})}
and
S\subset F\setminus{(H_{\pazocal{D}_{2}}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}_{2}})}
. The latter inclusions imply that
P
is a
transit cube
with respect to
\pazocal{D}_{1}
and we write
P\in\pazocal{D}_{1}^{\text{tr}}
, and analogously for
S
with respect to
\pazocal{D}_{2}
(we will specify the precise meaning of the terms
good
and
transit
in Remark
5.3
).
P,S
and all their dyadic children have
M
-
thin boundaries
(with respect to
\mu
), for some
M>0
dimensional constant. Recall that we say that a set
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
has
M
-thin boundary if

\mu\big(\big\{\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:\text{dist}(\overline{x},\partial X)\leq\tau\big\}\big)\leq M\tau^{n},\qquad\text{for all }\,\tau>0.

As one may expect, expressions of the form
\langle\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\mu}(\chi_{\Delta}b),(\chi_{\Delta}b)\rangle
are null if the kernel associated to the operator
\pazocal{P}
is anti-symmetric, as it occurs in
[
To3
]
with the Cauchy kernel, or in
[
Vo
]
with the vector Riesz kernel. If this is not the case, following the proofs of general non-homogeneous
Tb
-theorems and, more precisely, the arguments of
[
NTVo2
, §10.2]
or
[
HyMar
, §9]
, the latter expressions can be dealt with if we have
\|\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\lesssim 1
,
\|\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\lesssim 1
uniformly on
\varepsilon>0
, as well as the following weak boundedness property

|\langle\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\mu}(\chi_{Q}b),(\chi_{Q}b)\rangle|\lesssim\mu(\lambda Q),\quad\lambda>1,

that suffices to hold for cubes
Q
with
M
-thin boundary and contained in parallelepipeds
\Delta
of the form
P\cap S
, where
P
and
S
are as above. Such kind of restricted weak boundedness property for cubes with thin boundary is already checked in the proofs of
[
MPrTo
, Theorem 5.5]
and
[
MPr
, Theorem 4.3]
.

Our goal in this subsection will be to verify the above conditions by choosing a proper 1-Lipschitz function
\Lambda
which will depend on an additional exceptional set
\pazocal{S}
. If such additional properties hold, the proofs of the
Tb
-theorems found in
[
To3
, Ch.5]
and
[
Vo
]
can be adapted to our non anti-symmetric setting. To define the exceptional set
\pazocal{S}
, we will follow an analogous construction to that of
[
To3
, §5.2]
. We begin by writing

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\overline{x}\in F\,:\,\pazocal{P}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{x})>\alpha\big\},

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\overline{x}\in F\,:\,\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\nu,\ast}1(\overline{x})>\alpha\big\},

for a large constant
\alpha>0
to be chosen below. For the moment, let us say that
\alpha\gg c_{1}L
. We also write,

\displaystyle\text{for }\,\overline{x}\in\pazocal{S}_{1}^{\prime}:\quad e_{1}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle:=\text{sup}\big\{\varepsilon>0\,:\,|\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{x})|>\alpha\big\},

\displaystyle\text{for }\,\overline{x}\in\pazocal{S}_{2}^{\prime}:\quad e_{2}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle:=\text{sup}\big\{\varepsilon>0\,:\,|\pazocal{P}_{\nu,\varepsilon}^{\ast}1(\overline{x})|>\alpha\big\}.

If
\overline{x}\in F\setminus{(\pazocal{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\pazocal{S}_{2}^{\prime})}
we convey
e_{1}(\overline{x})=e_{2}(\overline{x}):=0
. We define also

\pazocal{S}_{1}:=\bigcup_{\overline{x}\in\pazocal{S}_{1}^{\prime}}B_{e_{1}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pazocal{S}_{2}:=\bigcup_{\overline{x}\in\pazocal{S}_{2}^{\prime}}B_{e_{2}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x}),

as well as the exceptional set

(5.13)

\pazocal{S}:=\pazocal{S}_{1}\cup\pazocal{S}_{2}.

Let us first show that for any
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,
\mu(\pazocal{S}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}})
is small if
\alpha
is taken big enough. The proof of the following lemma is analogous to that of
[
To3
, Lemma 5.2]
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\overline{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
\pazocal{D}:=\pazocal{D}(\overline{x}_{0})
. Then, for
\alpha>0
big enough,

\mu(\pazocal{S}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}})\leq\frac{4c_{5}}{\alpha}\mu(F).

Proof.

Let
\overline{y}\in\pazocal{S}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}}
and assume, for example,
\overline{y}\in\pazocal{S}_{1}
. Then
\overline{y}\in B_{e_{1}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x})
for some
\overline{x}\in\pazocal{S}_{1}^{\prime}
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})
be such that
|\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\nu|>\alpha
and
\overline{y}\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x})
. Observe that

\displaystyle\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\nu(\overline{x})-\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\big(\chi_{B_{2\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}(\overline{y})}\nu\big)\overline{x})\big\rvert+\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\big(\chi_{B_{2\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}(\overline{y})}\nu\big)(\overline{y})\big\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+c_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{B_{2\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}(\overline{y})}}|P(\overline{x}-\overline{z})-P(\overline{y}-\overline{z})|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{z}).

Since
\overline{y}\not\in H_{\pazocal{D}}
, the first two terms are bounded above by

c_{1}\frac{\mu\big(B_{2\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}(\overline{y})\big)}{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})^{n}}\leq 2^{n}Lc_{1}.

Regarding the third term, if
A
is the CZ constant of
P
from property
(c)
of
[
MPr
, Lemma 2.1]
, integration over annuli and using again that
\overline{y}\not\in H_{\pazocal{D}}
yield

c_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{B_{2\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}(\overline{y})}}|P(\overline{x}-\overline{z})-P(\overline{y}-\overline{z})|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\overline{z})\lesssim 2^{n}ALc_{1}.

Therefore, naming
\kappa:=2^{n}Lc_{1}(2+A)
, we have
\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\nu(\overline{x})-\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert\leq\kappa
, and thus

\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon_{0}(\overline{x})}\nu(\overline{y})\big\rvert>\alpha-\kappa,

which implies, in particular,
\pazocal{P}_{\ast}\nu(\overline{y})>\alpha-\kappa
. If we have had
\overline{y}\in\pazocal{S}_{2}
we would have obtained the same bound for
\pazocal{P}_{\ast}^{\ast}\nu(\overline{y})
, since
P
and
P^{\ast}
share the same CZ constants. In any case, pick
\alpha\geq 2\kappa
and observe that

\displaystyle\mu(\pazocal{S}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\pazocal{S}_{1}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu+\int_{\pazocal{S}_{2}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\alpha}\int_{F\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}}}\pazocal{P}_{\ast}\nu\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu+\frac{2}{\alpha}\int_{F\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}}}}\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\ast}\nu\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu\leq\frac{4c_{5}}{\alpha}\mu(F).

∎

One of the implications of the above lemma is the following: by setting
\delta_{1}:=(1+\delta_{0})/2
(where
\delta_{0}
is the parameter appearing in Lemma
5.2
), we have
\delta_{0}<\delta_{1}<1
, and choosing
\alpha:=\max\{2\kappa,8c_{5}/(1-\delta_{0})\}
, we get

\mu\big(H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}\big)+\mu\big(\pazocal{S}\setminus{H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}}\big)\leq\delta_{1}\mu(F),\qquad\forall\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}.

With this in mind, and defining the
total exceptional set

W_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}:=H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}\cup T_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}\cup\pazocal{S},

one obtains

(5.14)

\mu\big(F\setminus{W_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x})}}\big)\geq(1-\delta_{1})\mu(F),

which is a necessary inequality in order to carry out the final probabilistic argument in the proof of the
Tb
-theorem (see
[
To3
, §5.11.2]
).

The exceptional set
\pazocal{S}
exhibits additional important properties regarding suppressed kernels (see subsection
3.1
), provided their associated 1-Lipschitz function
\Lambda
satisfies certain conditions. In order to prove them, we present two preliminary results. Fix
\sigma
any finite Borel measure in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to[0,\infty)
a 1-Lipschitz function. The next lemma admits an analogous proof to that of
[
Vo
, Lemma 8.3]
.

Lemma 5.11
.

For any
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and any
\varepsilon\geq\Lambda(\overline{x})
,

\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon}\sigma(\overline{x})-\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\sigma(\overline{x})\big\rvert\lesssim\sup_{r\geq\Lambda(\overline{x})}\frac{|\sigma|(B_{r}(\overline{x}))}{r^{n}}.

The same result holds changing
\pazocal{P}
by
\pazocal{P}^{\ast}
.

The following lemma with an analogous proof to that of
[
To3
, Lemma 5.5]
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
r_{0}>0
such that
\mu(B_{r}(\overline{x}))\leq Lr^{n}
for
r\geq r_{0}
, as well as
|\pazocal{P}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{x})|\leq\alpha
and
|\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}\nu(\overline{x})|\leq\alpha
for
\varepsilon\geq r_{0}
. If
\Lambda(\overline{x})\geq r_{0}
, then

\big\rvert\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\nu\big(\overline{x})\rvert\lesssim\alpha+c_{1}L\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big\rvert\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\nu\big(\overline{x})\rvert\lesssim\alpha+c_{1}L,

uniformly on
\varepsilon>0
.

Bearing in mind the above result, we choose our 1-Lipschitz function
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to[0,\infty)
to satisfy, for some
\overline{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,

\Lambda(\overline{x})\geq\text{dist}\big(\overline{x},\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{\big(H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x}_{0})}\cup\pazocal{S}\big)}\big).

This choice is consistent with the Lipschitz functions
\Lambda
that appear in the proofs of the
Tb
-theorems presented in
[
To3
, Ch.5]
and
[
Vo
]
, in the sense that they are constructed to ensure that the previous inequality is satisfied. This way, since
H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x}_{0})}\cup\pazocal{S}
contains all non-Ahlfors balls and all the balls
B_{e_{1}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x})
,
B_{e_{2}(\overline{x})}(\overline{x})
for
\overline{x}\in F
, we have

\Lambda(\overline{x})\geq\max\{\pazocal{R}(\overline{x}),e_{1}(\overline{x}),e_{2}(\overline{x})\},

where
\pazocal{R}(\overline{x})
is the Ahlfors radius defined in (
5.5
). So by the definition of
\pazocal{S}
and choosing
r_{0}:=\max\{\pazocal{R}(\overline{x}),e_{1}(\overline{x}),e_{2}(\overline{x})\}
, Lemma
5.2
yields

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
\overline{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to[0,\infty)
a 1-Lipschitz function such that
\Lambda(\overline{x})\geq\text{{dist}}\big(\overline{x},\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus{\big(H_{\pazocal{D}(\overline{x}_{0})}\cup\pazocal{S}\big)}\big)
for all
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. Then,

\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\ast}\nu(\overline{x})\leq c_{\Lambda}\quad\text{and}\quad\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\ast}^{\ast}\nu(\overline{x})\leq c_{\Lambda},\quad\forall\overline{x}\in F,

with
c_{\Lambda}
depending only on
c_{1},c_{5},L
and
\delta_{0}
.

Hence, if we choose such a
\Lambda
, we have
\|\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\lesssim 1
and
\|\pazocal{P}^{\ast}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\lesssim 1
uniformly on
\varepsilon>0
. So we are left to verify the weak boundedness property for cubes with
M
-thin boundary contained in the parallelepipeds presented at the beginning of this subsection. In order to do it we will need two auxiliary results, similar to those found in
[
MPr
, §3]
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
\varphi
be a function supported on a cube
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Then,
\pazocal{P}_{\Lambda}(\varphi\nu)
is a locally integrable function and moreover, if
\mu(Q)\lesssim\ell(Q)^{n}
, there exists
\overline{x}_{0}\in\frac{1}{4}Q
and a constant
c_{0}
such that
